# Backlog

One row per work package. Status: `todo`, `in-progress`, `review`, `done`.
Rows are added by the supervisor; the format of a WP is in `docs/AGENTS.md`.
Milestones and tracks are in `docs/ROADMAP.md`.

| WP-021 | T-IR | `available_externally` + `alwaysinline` bodies: producers mark them, `hod` emits a local copy and redirects the module's calls (design 0001 point 6); test with libstdc++'s extern-template cases; widened 2026-11-12: hod accepts the module as the front end emits it, before kiln's -O0 `opt` step (about 190 ms, 17% of the compile of a standard-library-heavy unit, when the only trigger is `alwaysinline`): `alwaysinline` is an out-of-line call, bodies whose meaning depends on inlining are declined by name, a census of pre-opt modules Landed: `crates/hod/src/session/inlining.rs`, `crates/hod_cg/src/emit/copy.rs`, `spec/cli.md` "Always-inline", `spec/codegen.md` §9.7.2, `spec/landed/ir/WP-021.md`, design 0001 point 6 updated: an `available_externally` body that is not `alwaysinline` is verified, dropped and referenced externally; one that is `alwaysinline` and called directly gets a local copy `<name>.alwaysinline` (transitively; direct calls only, an address taken still names the external symbol; a body nothing calls is never compiled). Declined by name when a call may have been inlined: `alwaysinline:frame-address`, `:return-address`, `:stacksave-escapes`, `:returns-twice`, `:returns-local-address`, `:frame-register-asm`; the other -O0 `opt` triggers (coroutines, instrument-function entry, alloc-token) stay refused. Under `--strict` an unknown option exits 2. Pre-opt forms against `opt default<O0>` + `llc` over kiln's tests: 3,954 pass and 0 fail on LLVM 22, 3,771 and 0 on 19; 2,484 of the 2,539 modules that build post-opt build pre-opt (50 coroutines, 4 correct `frame-address` declines, 1 NEON). kiln's regex unit pre-opt: hod 0.06 s and 15 MB against `opt` + `llc` 0.42 s and 79 MB; +3.4% `.text` (bodies the inliner would delete); run time unchanged within noise. Scripts `scripts/preopt-corpus.sh`, `scripts/preopt-census.sh`. Left: the check is on whenever any `alwaysinline` is in the module (the readers do not record call-site attributes); discarded bodies are still parsed (`crates/hod/src/front.rs`); x86-64. | WP-003, WP-004 | done |

| WP-209 | T-X64 | **The large code model (`-mcmodel=large`), x86-64 first, then AArch64.** kiln writes the flag as the module flag `Code Model` 4 (with `Large Data Threshold` 0 on x86-64) and hod declines every such module (`module:code-model-large`, `x64:code-model`, `a64:code-model`), so a build with the flag is all fallback. Then x86-64 emits each as `llc` does: no reference assumes a 32-bit displacement to a symbol, to the GOT or between sections; the encoder and the object writer gain the relocations and section flags they lack. Then AArch64 (`movz`/`movk` with `R_AARCH64_MOVW_UABS_G0_NC` to `G3` outside PIC; what `llc` rejects stays refused by name). Checked by `irdiff` on both LLVM versions over tests that hold every reference kind, by relocation and section-flag comparison against `llc`, and by execution of programs linked with a linker script that places text, data and the GOT more than 4 GiB apart (ld.bfd, lld and mold where each supports it), plain and with `-g`, with exceptions thrown and caught across such objects; a sample of corpus modules rebuilt with the flag set through `--code-model=large` agrees with `llc` under execution; small-model output byte-identical. `module:code-model-medium` and `-kernel` on x86-64 are judged in the census: built if the large work covers them, refused by name otherwise. Landed: Large code model: `-code-model=large` and module flag `Code Model` 4 on x86-64 (static, PIC, PIE) and on AArch64 outside PIC, as `llc` 19 and 22 build them at `-O0` (`spec/codegen.md` 5.6.36). x86-64 gets the `.ltext`, `.ldata`, `.lbss` and `.lrodata` sections, `movabs` and GOTPC64, GOTOFF64 and GOT64 sequences, calls through `r11`, and large unwind and LSDA tables; AArch64 gets `movz`/`movk` addresses and large unwind tables. AArch64 large PIC is refused as `module:code-model-large-pic` (`llc` itself builds small PIC there and aborts on TLS), `a64:code-model-large-tls` for TLS models other than local exec, medium and kernel by name. Run the suite (`libcxx/test/std` and `libcxx/test/libcxx`) by the recipe of `docs/status/libcxx-hod.md` with `-g` added to the compile flags: first the kiln + `llc` control with `-g`, then hybrid with the census on, natively on AArch64 and for x86-64 (cross, tests under qemu, or the hosted sweep's workflow with a `-g` input, whichever the existing scripts support with least change). Then strict. A sample of the `-g` objects (at least 200 per target, spread over the directories) is compared with `llc`'s by `cargo xtask debug trees` and `llvm-dwarfdump --verify`; differences are classified by the class names of `spec/dwarf.md`. Refusal names and test differences with one cause and a small fix are fixed in this package with a test each; the rest are listed as rows to file. Result in `docs/status/libcxx-hod-debug.md` (and `-x64`), the recipe in the scripts beside the existing ones. Test executables and objects are deleted as the run goes: the volume keeps 80 GB free. Part 1 landed: hod writes a static member whose constant is wider than 64 bits as a block of its bytes, as `llc` does, in abbreviation families 115 and 116 for DWARF 5 and 4 (`spec/dwarf.md` 6.28): all 55 `debug:die-unsupported` refusals of the native libc++ `-g` run are gone. hod writes the retained variables and labels of an inlined subprogram as abstract entities, in `llc` 22's order, for functions only inlined and for functions also placed out of line (`spec/dwarf.md` 6.29): the `debug:retained-nodes` refusal is gone; an import scoped in a block, and retained imports or labels of a function also placed out of line, stay refused. `cargo xtask debug trees` fails on `llvm-dwarfdump` output past the 16 MiB capture cap instead of reporting a false difference, and reads DIE trees through files. DIE trees match `llc` on all twelve retained-node variants, and none of the 55 libc++ modules differs from `llc`. Landed: libc++'s test suite (9247 tests) with `-g` through hod on both targets. The self-check of a split build sets a seed apart as `llc19-int128-last-register` only when `gcc` and `llc` 22 agree with each other and `llc` 19 does not. Landed: hod lowers `llvm.x86.tpause`, `llvm.x86.umwait` and `llvm.x86.umonitor` as `llc -O0` does (`spec/codegen.md` 5.6.31; encoder forms `tpause_r`, `umwait_r`, `umonitor_r`, `spec/mc-x64.md`) in a function with the `waitpkg` feature, and refuses each by its own name without it, where `llc` 19 and 22 stop with "Cannot select". | WP-268 | T-H | **The hosted real-projects job for abseil on AArch64.** The job failed twice on the hosted runner (a failed step with no log; then a cancel 100 minutes in, with the runner slowing down after the first test phase, in the control's phase too). The cause is found; a phase that runs a project's programs leaves nothing running behind it, and what it had to kill is reported. Landed: No process was left running after the control's tests on the development host, so leftovers are not the cause. The hosted data shows the programs with death tests 50 to 6000 times slower on the runner and the configure after a test phase 100 times slower; the inferred cause, to be confirmed by the next hosted run, is the runner's crash-dump handler started for each crashing child. Every phase of the lane runs with a core limit of 1 byte, at which the kernel starts no handler (`spec/harness.md` "No core dumps"), in a session of its own whose leftovers are killed, named in the phase's log and counted in `report.json` without changing a verdict ("A phase's leftovers"); `oss-run.sh` logs the core pattern, the limit, the load, the memory and the disk. `scripts/oss/oss-report-selftest.py` 51 of 51. | WP-269 | T-CG | **The constant fold never refuses a function.** Found by the hosted libc++ run and a real-project lane at the 1.0 candidate: WP-261 refused a function in which `llc`'s pass would also rewrite an `invoke` of a `nounwind` callee (`intrinsic:const-fold-nounwind-invoke`: 10 `std::basic_string` tests of libc++ on each target, 21 of 159 translation units of one project) or one of three sibling cases. A function hod compiled before is not refused: in each of the four cases hod keeps the instruction as written, which is what it emitted before WP-261, and the blocks its edges reach stay emitted. hod's object can hold a landing pad or a block that `llc`'s does not; the specification says so. Landed: `hod_cg::const_fold` keeps, in a function whose blocks the fold changes, a store to or a call or `invoke` through `null`, an `invoke` through `undef`, an `invoke` of a `nounwind` or `noreturn` callee and an `indirectbr` on a `blockaddress` as written, with all their edges; the four names `intrinsic:const-fold-{null-access,nounwind-invoke,noreturn-invoke,indirectbr}` of WP-261 no longer exist and the fold never refuses (`spec/codegen.md` 5.6.8). hod's object holds a superset of `llc`'s blocks there; its undefined symbols on the three modules of the finding are `llc`'s or fewer, plus `__gxx_personality_v0` where a landing pad is kept. Tests on both targets (`crates/hod/tests/const_fold_blocks.rs`: the four kept cases compile, link and print what `llc`'s program prints; the reduced module of the finding). 792 objects of modules the previous candidate compiled are the same bytes. Record: `spec/landed/codegen/WP-269.md`. | WP-261 | done |

## M1 and later

Rows are written when the interfaces they depend on are reviewed. Planned
shape:

- T-CG: the M2 instruction groups (exceptions, TLS, atomics, vectors,
  `musttail`), then the same groups for x86-64.
- T-EH: LSDA.
- T-DW: line tables; unit and subprogram skeleton; types; variables;
  split DWARF.
- T-XF: coroutine splitting (design WP first).
- T-X64: encoder WPs mirroring WP-012/013.
- T-H: `xtask bisect`; L5 ABI grid generator; kiln corpus and libc++
  runners in hybrid and strict mode; fuzzing drivers.
